I've always had a good motor, but I wouldn't say I've ever had a period quite like this. I mean, sure, everyone has a bad day here and there, but... no, never anything persistent. I've always just kept my head down and kept working. I just get my regular shut-eye and get back to work.\n\nDoctor: Has this period of increased drive caused serious problems in your work, relationships, finances, or ability to function?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose it has. I actually... I ended up getting pulled from work recently. There was a safety incident, so they took me off the floor. And at home, well, my wife and the rest of the family have basically stopped trusting me with any of the decisions. They won't let me handle the money, or the driving, or even the plans for the move. They just said things had gotten too out of hand.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have your thoughts been moving faster than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. I mean, I'm thinking about what needs to get done—the packing, the move, all that—but... no, my mind isn't racing or anything like that. Just regular thinking, same as always.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you felt unusually happy or euphoric?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say happy, exactly. More like... well, I've just felt like everybody needs to either get on board with my plans or get out of my way. One minute I'm joking around, feeling ten feet tall, like I can handle everything. And then the next minute, I'm snapping at my wife or my boy just because they asked some little question. It's just... frustrating when people aren't keeping up.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's just that right now everything needs doing all at once, so I'm stepping up to get it done.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling unusually energized and driven like this?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... I'd say this has been going on for about a month now. Yeah, right about a month. It started right around the time we were forced to start leaving the old house. There was just so much to do, so I got to work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt persistently depressed?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. I mean, sure, everyone has a bad day here and there, but... no, never anything persistent. I've always just kept my head down and kept working. I've never really been the type to sit around feeling down like that.\n\nDoctor: Since this energized period began, have you needed less sleep than usual while still feeling rested?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, I've always been an early riser—you get used to a certain schedule working the grinders all those years. I might be up a bit earlier lately just because there's so much packing and moving to do, but... no, I wouldn't say I need less sleep. I just get my regular shut-eye and get back to work.\n\nDoctor: Has this period of increased drive caused serious problems in your work, relationships, finances, or ability to function?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose it has. I actually... I ended up getting pulled from work recently. There was a safety incident, so they took me off the floor. And at home, well, my wife and the rest of the family have basically stopped trusting me with any of the decisions. They won't let me handle the money, or the driving, or even the plans for the move. They just said things had gotten too out of hand.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have your thoughts been moving faster than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. I mean, I'm thinking about what needs to get done—the packing, the move, all that—but... no, my mind isn't racing or anything like that. Just regular thinking, same as always.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you felt unusually happy or euphoric?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say happy, exactly. More like... well, I've just felt like everybody needs to either get on board with my plans or get out of my way. One minute I'm joking around, feeling ten feet tall, like I can handle everything. And then the next minute, I'm snapping at my wife or my boy just because they asked some little question. It's just... frustrating when people aren't keeping up.\n\nDoctor: When you are not dealing with the move or other related pressures, do you still feel unusually driven and irritable?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess I'm just focused on the move right now, so it's hard to separate the two. I don't really notice feeling this way when there isn't something going on that I need to handle.\n\nDoctor: Before the move became a problem, were you already feeling unusually driven or irritable?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Before all this moving stuff started, I felt pretty normal. I didn't have this... urgency, or whatever you want to call it. It really just started when we decided we had to pack up and go.\n\nDoctor: Have you been talking faster than usual during this period?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I have. It's like... once I start explaining what needs doing, the words just keep coming. My family actually tells me I talk right over them. They say I'm going so fast they can't even get a word in to tell me to stop.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you felt unusually powerful or believed you had special abilities or talents beyond your usual self-confidence?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't felt anything like that. I mean... I don't think I have any special abilities or anything. I'm just trying to get this move sorted out. I know how to get the work done and get things packed, but that's just regular common sense, you know? Not some kind of superpower.\n\nDoctor: Since this energized period began, have you done anything unusually risky or impulsive that you would not normally do?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I have. I went ahead and put deposits down on some garage work and new equipment without talking it over with my wife first. And... I just kept driving back and forth between the houses trying to get it all done. I was also rushing my jobs at the plant. People actually warned me I was taking chances, but I just kept pushing through it anyway.\n\nDoctor: If the move were temporarily put on hold, would you still feel unusually driven and irritable?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. It's hard to say. The move is what's on my plate right now, so that's what I'm focused on. If it got put on hold... I honestly can't tell you how I'd feel. I just know right now I've got to get it done.\n\nDoctor: Since the move began, have you felt persistently anxious?\n\nPatient: Anxious? No, I wouldn't really say I've felt anxious. I've been wound up and irritable, sure, trying to get everything done... but not worried or fearful, if that's what you mean. Just focused on the work in front of me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's just that right now everything needs doing all at once, so I'm stepping up to get it done.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling unusually energized and driven like this?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... I'd say this has been going on for about a month now. Yeah, right about a month. It started right around the time we were forced to start leaving the old house. There was just so much to do, so I got to work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt persistently depressed?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. I mean, sure, everyone has a bad day here and there, but... no, never anything persistent. I've always just kept my head down and kept working. I've never really been the type to sit around feeling down like that.\n\nDoctor: Since this energized period began, have you needed less sleep than usual while still feeling rested?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, I've always been an early riser—you get used to a certain schedule working the grinders all those years. I might be up a bit earlier lately just because there's so much packing and moving to do, but... no, I wouldn't say I need less sleep. I just get my regular shut-eye and get back to work.\n\nDoctor: Has this period of increased drive caused serious problems in your work, relationships, finances, or ability to function?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose it has. I actually... I ended up getting pulled from work recently. There was a safety incident, so they took me off the floor. And at home, well, my wife and the rest of the family have basically stopped trusting me with any of the decisions. They won't let me handle the money, or the driving, or even the plans for the move. They just said things had gotten too out of hand.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have your thoughts been moving faster than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. I mean, I'm thinking about what needs to get done—the packing, the move, all that—but... no, my mind isn't racing or anything like that. Just regular thinking, same as always.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you felt unusually happy or euphoric?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say happy, exactly. More like... well, I've just felt like everybody needs to either get on board with my plans or get out of my way. One minute I'm joking around, feeling ten feet tall, like I can handle everything. And then the next minute, I'm snapping at my wife or my boy just because they asked some little question. It's just... frustrating when people aren't keeping up.\n\nDoctor: When you are not dealing with the move or other related pressures, do you still feel unusually driven and irritable?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess I'm just focused on the move right now, so it's hard to separate the two. I don't really notice feeling this way when there isn't something going on that I need to handle.\n\nDoctor: Before the move became a problem, were you already feeling unusually driven or irritable?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Before all this moving stuff started, I felt pretty normal. I didn't have this... urgency, or whatever you want to call it. It really just started when we decided we had to pack up and go.\n\nDoctor: Have you been talking faster than usual during this period?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I have. It's like... once I start explaining what needs doing, the words just keep coming. My family actually tells me I talk right over them. They say I'm going so fast they can't even get a word in to tell me to stop.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you felt unusually powerful or believed you had special abilities or talents beyond your usual self-confidence?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't felt anything like that. I mean... I don't think I have any special abilities or anything. I'm just trying to get this move sorted out. I know how to get the work done and get things packed, but that's just regular common sense, you know? Not some kind of superpower.\n\nDoctor: Since this energized period began, have you done anything unusually risky or impulsive that you would not normally do?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I have. I went ahead and put deposits down on some garage work and new equipment without talking it over with my wife first. And... I just kept driving back and forth between the houses trying to get it all done. I was also rushing my jobs at the plant. People actually warned me I was taking chances, but I just kept pushing through it anyway.\n\nDoctor: If the move were temporarily put on hold, would you still feel unusually driven and irritable?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. It's hard to say. The move is what's on my plate right now, so that's what I'm focused on. If it got put on hold... I honestly can't tell you how I'd feel. I just know right now I've got to get it done.\n\nDoctor: Since the move began, have you felt persistently anxious?\n\nPatient: Anxious? No, I wouldn't really say I've felt anxious. I've been wound up and irritable, sure, trying to get everything done... but not worried or fearful, if that's what you mean. Just focused on the work in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a specific time since the move began when you felt unusually driven or irritable while you were not thinking about or working on the move?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... since we started this whole process, the move is pretty much all I think about. If I'm not doing my shift at the plant, I'm either at the new house or the old one. So I can't really pull up a specific time where I felt that wound up about something else. It's just all tied to getting this done.\n\nDoctor: If all moving-related tasks were removed for an entire day, would you still feel unusually driven or irritable?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know how to answer that. I mean, I haven't had a day like that since this whole thing started. If I had a whole day with nothing to do for the move... I guess I'm not sure if I'd still feel this way or not. I just haven't experienced it to tell you.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I might be up a bit earlier lately just because there's so much packing and moving to do, but... no, I wouldn't say I need less sleep. I just get my regular shut-eye and get back to work.\n\nDoctor: Has this period of increased drive caused serious problems in your work, relationships, finances, or ability to function?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose it has. I actually... I ended up getting pulled from work recently. There was a safety incident, so they took me off the floor. And at home, well, my wife and the rest of the family have basically stopped trusting me with any of the decisions. They won't let me handle the money, or the driving, or even the plans for the move. They just said things had gotten too out of hand.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have your thoughts been moving faster than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. I mean, I'm thinking about what needs to get done—the packing, the move, all that—but... no, my mind isn't racing or anything like that. Just regular thinking, same as always.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you felt unusually happy or euphoric?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say happy, exactly. More like... well, I've just felt like everybody needs to either get on board with my plans or get out of my way. One minute I'm joking around, feeling ten feet tall, like I can handle everything. And then the next minute, I'm snapping at my wife or my boy just because they asked some little question. It's just... frustrating when people aren't keeping up.\n\nDoctor: When you are not dealing with the move or other related pressures, do you still feel unusually driven and irritable?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess I'm just focused on the move right now, so it's hard to separate the two. I don't really notice feeling this way when there isn't something going on that I need to handle.\n\nDoctor: Before the move became a problem, were you already feeling unusually driven or irritable?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Before all this moving stuff started, I felt pretty normal. I didn't have this... urgency, or whatever you want to call it. It really just started when we decided we had to pack up and go.\n\nDoctor: Have you been talking faster than usual during this period?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I have. It's like... once I start explaining what needs doing, the words just keep coming. My family actually tells me I talk right over them. They say I'm going so fast they can't even get a word in to tell me to stop.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you felt unusually powerful or believed you had special abilities or talents beyond your usual self-confidence?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't felt anything like that. I mean... I don't think I have any special abilities or anything. I'm just trying to get this move sorted out. I know how to get the work done and get things packed, but that's just regular common sense, you know? Not some kind of superpower.\n\nDoctor: Since this energized period began, have you done anything unusually risky or impulsive that you would not normally do?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I have. I went ahead and put deposits down on some garage work and new equipment without talking it over with my wife first. And... I just kept driving back and forth between the houses trying to get it all done. I was also rushing my jobs at the plant. People actually warned me I was taking chances, but I just kept pushing through it anyway.\n\nDoctor: If the move were temporarily put on hold, would you still feel unusually driven and irritable?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. It's hard to say. The move is what's on my plate right now, so that's what I'm focused on. If it got put on hold... I honestly can't tell you how I'd feel. I just know right now I've got to get it done.\n\nDoctor: Since the move began, have you felt persistently anxious?\n\nPatient: Anxious? No, I wouldn't really say I've felt anxious. I've been wound up and irritable, sure, trying to get everything done... but not worried or fearful, if that's what you mean. Just focused on the work in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a specific time since the move began when you felt unusually driven or irritable while you were not thinking about or working on the move?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... since we started this whole process, the move is pretty much all I think about. If I'm not doing my shift at the plant, I'm either at the new house or the old one. So I can't really pull up a specific time where I felt that wound up about something else. It's just all tied to getting this done.\n\nDoctor: If all moving-related tasks were removed for an entire day, would you still feel unusually driven or irritable?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know how to answer that. I mean, I haven't had a day like that since this whole thing started. If I had a whole day with nothing to do for the move... I guess I'm not sure if I'd still feel this way or not. I just haven't experienced it to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your level of activity and irritability has been far greater than what the move realistically requires?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, moving a whole house after living there for years, it takes a lot of work. There's packing, hauling boxes, fixing things up... it's a massive job. So, no, I think the way I've been pushing myself makes sense for how much there is to do."
